$data_generation_basis
[1] "HMAC under the noise root of SHA-256 digests of canonically ordered distinct person_id records with unit weight in fixed public identifier buckets, recomputed inside each fixed-synopsis request's own read snapshot; nothing is cached between requests and the digest is never released"

$data_generation_residual
[1] "The generation follows the person set only. Clinical-only changes that leave the person set unchanged (in-place edits, added or removed records of existing persons) keep the generation: the fixed-synopsis components whose bounded contributions changed redraw one by one (their noise is also keyed on their realised contributions). Comparing answers can reveal component change/equality patterns; auxiliary information can reveal more. This is not a joint guarantee over edits. Each new person set is another data state whose fixed synopses are another disclosure."

[1] "Complete canonical interval-presence vector; coarse persons are never sums of fine-bin persons. Feasible radial projection toward a concave event-mass anchor enforces public bounds and interval constraints; retreat toward that anchor can add bias. This is not an L2 projection."


$components[[1]]$views
[1] "time_scale 365: A nodes; 30: 30-day bins [30 j, 30 j + 30) of [-3650, 3650) (edge bins 20 days) as sums of B atoms; 1: days of [-365, 365) (C atoms) with tails (-Inf, -365) and [365, Inf) as sums of A nodes; event views sum displayed nodes of one draw; recurrent persons select canonical interval-presence coordinates and are never added across bins"



$interval_contract
[1] "bins [b_i, b_i+1) with an open last bin; the first bin is the open underflow bin on scales without a public lower bound"

$quantile_method
[1] "inverse of the displayed protected histogram's CDF, linear within finite bins; quantiles in an open tail bin report its finite edge (censored); each owner's quantile_intervals give every quantile's bin interval and open/censored flags"
